Unaudited results for the nine
months ended 30 September 2026
29 October 2026
Reliable energy,
limitless potential

Interim Management Report
Lagos and London, 29 October 2026: Seplat Energy Plc (“Seplat Energy” or “the Company”), a leading Nigerian independent energy company
listed on both the Nigerian Exchange Group and the London Stock Exchange, announces its unaudited results for the nine months ended 30
September 2026.
Summary
Strong production and price realisations delivered 9M 2026 free cash flow of $749 million, further improving net leverage to c.0.14x TTM Net Debt/
EBITDA, and underpinning increased distributions. 3Q 2026 dividend raised by 58% sequentially to USD 19.0 cents/share, reflects the strong
operational and financial performance.
Operational highlights
• 9M 2026 working interest production averaged 144,999 boepd up 7% from 9M 2025 (135,636 boepd) with Onshore up 22%.
• 3Q 2026 working interest production averaged 155,801 boepd, up 13% YoY from 3Q 2025 (137,888 boepd) and up 5% sequentially.
• ANOH initial debt principal repayments completed during 3Q. First commercial gas sold via OB3 pipeline in September.
• Offshore, idle well restoration programme continued its strong performance, adding 33.7 kbopd and 176 MMscf/d gross JV production
capacity YTD from 41 wells. Of the 41 wells restored YTD, 4 are gas wells.
• Post period end, discovery of oil and gas across five reservoir intervals in the Aku-01 exploration well, in PPL 2A25. Discovery de-risks four
prospects on the license, one of which will be tested in a second exploration well that is now being planned for 2027.
• Carbon emissions intensity for the group: 37.4 kg CO/boe 14% lower YoY (9M 2025: 43.6 kg CO/boe). Onshore operated emissions
2 2
intensity reduced 34% YoY, reflecting the continued positive impact of our End of Routine Flaring programme.
Financial highlights
• Revenue $2,764 million up 27% on prior year (9M 2025: $2,177 million). 9M 2026 average realised oil price $96.19/bbl, an $8.12/bbl premium
to average Brent price, 3Q 2026 average realised oil price of $100.44/bbl, a $10.21/bbl premium to average Brent price.
• Adjusted EBITDA of $1,530 million, up 38% on prior year (9M 2025: $1,111.9 million).
• Net income increased to $260.2 million, up 174% YoY. Earnings per share USD 42 cents, up 172% YoY (9M 2025: USD 16 cents).
• Cash generated from operations of $1,478.7 million, up 6% on prior year (9M 2025: $1,395.4 million).
• Balance sheet remains strong, end-Sept cash at bank $556.9 million (FY 2025: $332.3 million).
• Net Debt at end-Sept of $234.9 million down 65% since YE 2025 ($673.3 million). Net Debt/EBITDA improves to 0.14x from 0.53x at YE 2025.
• On 27 October, the Company repaid the $100 million balance on the APF. As such, all debt raised to acquire MPNU in 2024 has been repaid.
Corporate Update & 2026 Outlook
• Sale of a 10% interest in NNPCL-SEPNU JV to NNPC Ltd expected to complete during 4Q 2026. As previously disclosed, following
completion, the Company intends to pay a Transaction dividend of approximately half the consideration (USD 23.3 cents/share, $140
million).
• 3Q 2026 declared dividend of USD 19.0 cents/share ($114 million), consisting of USD 5.0 c/shr core and USD 14.0 c/shr special dividend.
Total dividend declared YTD USD 40.0 cents/share. FY 2026 dividend guidance of $270 million (USD 45.0 cents/share), is retained.
• 2026 guidance updated to reflect progress year to date:
• Production guidance narrowed to the upper end at 145-155 kboepd (from 135-155 kboepd), reflecting strong YTD performance.
• Capex guidance lowered to $280-$340 million (from $360-440 million), primarily due to phasing, given the late arrival of the offshore rig.
• Unit operating cost guidance increased to $15.5-$16.5/boe (from $14.5-$15.5/boe), due to ongoing costs related to Yoho.
Effiong Okon, Chief Executive Officer, said:
“In my first update as CEO, I am delighted to report another quarter of robust production growth, averaging over
155 kboepd in 3Q 2026, which puts us firmly on track to meet the upper half of our full year guidance. This was
delivered in a period of continued high crude prices, we realised $100.4/bbl in the quarter, a full $10.2/bbl higher
than the average Brent price in the period, further enhancing our results.
Operationally, in our Onshore business, we achieved record gas production levels in the quarter, supported by
continued solid ANOH ramp-up alongside strong performance on our Western assets, and we have clear line of
sight to continued growth that will be underpinned by operational excellence as offshore gas production comes
back online. Yoho restoration project was completed during the quarter, although start-up has been delayed due
to pipeline damage that was identified and is being repaired, production is expected online in 4Q 2026. I’m
pleased to announce exploration success at Aku-01, a strong reminder of our rich portfolio of prospects and the
quality of the subsurface in Nigeria.
Our strong financial performance supports the interim dividend payment of USD 19.0 cents per share. When paid,
the 3Q 2026 dividend will lift cumulative dividends declared since our IPO to over $1.0 billion, and we are firmly on
track to return our second $1.0 billion to shareholders as we deliver on our 5 year plan. A clear demonstration of
the financial robustness of our business.
Seplat Energy Plc 1 9M 2026 Financial Results

As I take on the responsibilities of leading Seplat into its next phase of growth, based on my engagements in the
first three months on the job, I am inspired by the quality of our people, excited with the depth of portfolio growth
opportunities and encouraged by the strength of our balance sheet. We are making significant progress on
integration, and multiple initiatives are underway to further enhance our operational capabilities. With these
foundations in place, I believe that we are well positioned to deliver substantial growth, further reinforcing our
position as a leading energy provider in Nigeria, a region of increasing strategic importance to global energy
markets.”

About Seplat Energy
Seplat Energy Plc is Nigeria's leading indigenous energy company. It is listed on the Premium Board of the Nigerian Exchange Limited (NGX:
SEPLAT) and the Main Market of the London Stock Exchange (LSE: SEPL). Through our strategy to build a sustainable business and deliver energy
transition, we are transforming lives by delivering affordable, reliable and sustainable energy that drives social and economic prosperity.
Seplat Energy's portfolio consists of 11 PMLs, 17 PPLs and 5 OMLs in onshore and shallow water locations in the prolific Niger Delta region of
Nigeria, the majority of which we operate with partners including the Nigerian Government and other producers. Furthermore, we have an
operated interest in three export terminals including; the Qua Iboe export terminal, Yoho FSO, and Bonny River Terminal (BRT), and operate two
large offshore NGL recovery plants at Oso and EAP.
We operate three gas processing plants onshore, at Oben and Sapele on our Western Assets and the 300 MMscfd ANOH Gas Processing Plant
on our Eastern Assets, an integrated joint venture with NGIC. Combined, these gas facilities augment Seplat Energy's position as a leading
supplier of natural gas to the domestic power generation market.
For further information please refer to our website, https://www.seplatenergy.com/

ANOH production contribution represents Seplat’s upstream equity interest production. This sold product mix is used to calculate upstream wet gas revenue due to Seplat from AGPC.
In 9M 2026, average daily working interest production for the group increased by 7% to 144,999 boepd (9M 2025: 135,636 boepd), in-line with the
midpoint of the guidance range.
Our Onshore assets delivered strong production growth, up 22% YoY to 67,445 boepd (9M 2025: 55,298 boepd) driven by steadily improving
production output from ANOH, contributions from new wells brought onstream through the 2025 drilling programme in Elcrest, and reduced
production deferments across the Western assets during 2Q and 3Q 2026. Offshore assets average daily working interest production in the first
9 months of the year declined by 3% to 77,554 boepd (9M 2025: 80,337 boepd). While technical completion of the Yoho restoration project has
been achieved, restart has been deferred to 4Q 2026, details discussed below. In addition, commercial gas sales offshore were suspended
during 3Q pending completion of the planned sectional replacement of the accidentally damaged Oso-BRT pipeline, which is planned for end
2026.
Production deferments on the Onshore assets were 22% in 9M 2026 (9M 2025: 21%), reflecting the elevated deferments recorded in 1Q 2026
following the unplanned TFP outage. Operational performance improved significantly thereafter, with deferments reducing to 15% in 2Q and
further to 14% in 3Q 2026. Offshore deferments also improved to 16% in 9M 2026, from 20% in 9M 2025, benefiting from continuous investment
in asset integrity and reliability.

Group average daily working interest production in 3Q 2026 grew by 5% QoQ to 155,801 boepd (2Q 2026: 149,070 boepd), reflecting strong
operational performance, particularly on the Onshore Assets. Onshore production grew by 14% compared to the prior quarter, driven by improved
performance on our Western Assets and Elcrest, and the continued ramp up at ANOH. Offshore production decreased by 4% QoQ reflecting the
impact of the Oso–BRT pipeline leakage due to accidental third party damage, which curtailed NGL and gas production, and the delayed restart
of Yoho production, which continued to impact crude production.
HSE Performance
While our operated assets delivered another LTI-free quarter, tragically, a marine-related fatality occurred on 27 September 2026 at Gbetiokun, a
non-operated asset within the Elcrest joint venture. An investigation is underway, and further information will be provided upon its conclusion. The
incident serves as a stark reminder of the risks associated with our operations and reinforces our continued focus on safety leadership, risk
management and the protection of our workforce and contractors across both operated and non-operated assets.
Across our operated assets, we recorded 10.68 million LTI-free man-hours during the quarter and 29.44 million year-to-date.

In 3Q 2026, we reported zero Tier-1 and Tier-2 Loss of Primary Containment (LOPC). We continue to strengthen our spill response, regulatory
engagement, and remediation oversight, while maintaining our oil spill contingency plan to support effective management of spill scenarios
across our operations.
During the quarter, the Group achieved ISO 14001 Environmental Management System certification and successfully completed ISO 45001
surveillance audits, reinforcing its commitment to environmental stewardship, regulatory compliance, and international best practice in
occupational health and safety. The Group also completed its annual regulatory led Oil Spill Contingency Plan activation exercise and asset HSE
inspections, resulting in a year-on-year improvement in HSE compliance from 75% to 85%, with a target of achieving full compliance in
subsequent periods.
Emissions
Carbon emissions intensity across our operated onshore assets declined by 34% to 17.0 kgCO₂/boe in 9M 2026 (9M 2025: 25.9 kgCO₂/boe),
despite higher gas production. The improvement reflects continued benefits from the End of Routine Flaring programme, increased utilisation at
ANOH and initiatives to increase gas sales and reduce flaring. Offshore carbon emissions intensity declined by 10% to 46.1 kgCO₂/boe (9M 2025:
51.2 kgCO₂/boe), reflecting production optimisation and operational efficiency initiatives, alongside lower associated gas production following the
Oso-BRT pipeline outage.
Overall carbon emissions intensity across our operated assets reduced by 14% to 37.4 kgCO₂/boe in 9M 2026, from 43.6 kgCO₂/boe in 9M 2025.

Crude, Condensate & NGL Production
Working interest crude & condensate production from OMLs 67, 68, 70, and 104 averaged 64,677 bopd in 3Q 2026, marginally down 1% QoQ (2Q
2026: 65,028 bopd) with contributions from idle well restoration and ongoing asset optimisation activities across the offshore portfolio minimising
the impact of the delayed restart of the Yoho production platform. 9M 2026 production remained below 9M 2025 levels by 5% at 63,879 bopd
(9M 2025: 68,623 bopd), reflecting the continued impact of the Yoho outage.
Average daily working interest NGL production increased by 7% QoQ to 7,615 boepd in 3Q 2026 (2Q 2026: 7,131 boepd) after the decline
experienced in 2Q 2026 due to the remedial repairs undertaken during the quarter on the accidentally damaged Oso-BRT pipeline enabling
restoration of NGL processing at BRT in August. In spite of this, NGL production in 9M 2026 grew by 92% YoY to 8,175 bopd (9M 2025: 3,719
boepd) driven by the strong production of NGLs from the EAP complex after the reported Inlet Gas Exchanger (IGE) replacement project in 2025.
The non-operated Amenam-Kpono (A/K) field contributed 931 bopd of crude and condensate production during the quarter, up 34% compared
with 2Q 2026 (694 bopd of crude and condensate).
Idle well restoration
During the quarter, interventions and investigations were progressed on a further 17 idle wells, bringing the total number of wells worked on under
the programme to 41 wells in 9M 2026, against FY 2026 guidance of 50 wells. Of the 17 wells worked on in 3Q 2026, four previously were shut-in
gas wells restored ahead of Oso-BRT phase 1 completion. These four wells added gross production capacity of 176 MMscf/d. The remaining 13 oil
wells added 7.7 kbopd gross oil production capacity. Total incremental gross production capacity added year to date from 41 wells stands at 64.0
kboepd. Given year to date progress and the 4Q 2026 idle well programme plan, we are on track to exceed 2026 guidance of 50 wells.
The continued progress of the programme highlights the production potential within the existing asset portfolio and supports our strategy of
maximising production through targeted, capital-efficient well interventions.
Yoho production platform
Technical completion of the Yoho production platform restoration project was achieved during the quarter, with approximately 3.7 million man-
hours worked and zero lost-time injuries (LTIs) recorded. Key activities completed included major fabrication, piping, cable installation, riser repairs,
installation, alignment of the Gas Turbine Generator alternator, and restoration of power to the platform.
Production restart has however been deferred to 4Q 2026 following the identification of a leak on the 14-inch pipeline connecting the Yoho
platform to the FSO during restart activities. It has been determined that a section of the pipeline requires replacement, this work is underway and
targeting completion this quarter.
In parallel, we are progressing a temporary shuttle-tanker solution as an alternative evacuation route. Technical and commercial evaluations are
ongoing alongside engagement with the relevant regulatory authorities. The initiative provides an additional pathway to accelerate production
restoration while permanent repairs to the pipeline are completed
Drilling activities
The offshore drilling programme was impacted by the delays to the mobilisation of the Shelf Drilling Victory (SDV) jack-up rig, which was unable to
mobilise to Seplat and commence operations in 3Q 2026 as planned. The rig is currently operating in Nigeria, and we continue to work closely
with the contractor to secure mobilisation and minimise the impact on the drilling schedule.
In response, the Company is advancing multiple alternative rig options, targeting an additional unit to arrive in 1Q 2027. This is in addition to the
Shelf Drilling Odyssey, which is due to arrive in early 2027.
Asset integrity
Offshore production deferments improved to 16% in 9M 2026, compared with 20% in 9M 2025, supported by continued execution of the asset
integrity and reliability programme targeting maintenance, rehabilitation and equipment upgrade across the portfolio.
Looking ahead, ongoing reliability initiatives include the Mobiltherm Heater project revamp at QIT to address identified equipment integrity risks
and the repairs of the settling tank to support production continuity.
PIA Conversion
Following further regulatory engagement, NUPRC has indicated that the original conversion window has elapsed. The Group continues to engage
with NUPRC and other relevant stakeholders to assess available options for the offshore assets and determine the optimal long-term licence
framework.

Western Assets
Average daily working interest crude and condensate production on our Western Assets was 18,986 in 3Q 2026, broadly flat QoQ following the
strong recovery achieved in 2Q 2026 after the resumption of operations on the Trans Forcados Pipeline (TFP), which experienced a 38-day
unplanned third-party outage in 1Q 2026. The recovery recorded in 2Q was sustained throughout 3Q, supported by lower downtime across the
assets.
On a 9M 2026 basis, average daily working interest production declined marginally by 1% YoY at 16,811 bopd (9M 2025: 16,921 bopd). New wells
and the use of buffer tank facilities, which help to reduce production deferments associated with third-party infrastructure downtime, offset the
unplanned downtime on TFP.
Elcrest
Elcrest delivered a strong performance in 3Q 2026, with daily working interest crude and condensate production averaging 16,331 bopd, up 14%
QoQ (2Q 2026: 14,313 bopd). The improvement was driven by enhanced pipeline availability and the successful execution of evacuation route
debottlenecking initiatives. As a result of these initiatives asset deferments improved progressively during the year, declining from 34% in 1Q 2026
to 23% in 2Q and 19% in 3Q 2026.
Average daily working interest production in 9M 2026 improved by 30% YoY to 14,440 bopd (9M 2025: 11,142 bopd) due to strong contribution
from wells delivered under the 2025 and 2026 Elcrest drilling programme and improved production at Abiala driven by the commencement of
access to a third party custody transfer unit in 1Q 2026.
Eastern Assets
Eastern Assets delivered average daily working interest crude and condensate production of 3,887 bopd in 3Q 2026, a 21% increase from 2Q
2026 (3,205 bopd). The improvement was supported by stronger well performance and reduced third-party interruptions following the
deployment of buffer tanks. As a result, total deferment fell to 3.5% compared with 15.7% in 1Q 2026 and 8.1% in 2Q 2026, while third-party
deferment reduced to 1.4% from 6.0%in 1Q 2026 and 2.5% in 2Q 2026. This represents the lowest deferment level recorded during the year and
reflects the success of ongoing evacuation and infrastructure reliability initiatives.
Average daily working interest grew YoY by 19% to 3,352 bopd in 9M 2026 (9M 2025: 2,828 bopd) supported by factors already highlighted
above.
Post period end, the Company successfully discovered oil and gas in the Aku-01 exploration well. Preliminary internal estimates indicate a mid-
case gas in-place resource of 157 Bcf and oil in-place resource of 19 MMbbls. Encouragingly, an oil discovery was not part of the original well plan.
The well is currently being completed as a potential future production well, that will be tied-back to existing infrastructure, supporting early
monetization of the hydrocarbon resources. The discovery also de-risks four adjacent prospects. Seplat is now planning for a second exploration
in the Aku area in 2027. Seplat has a 40% operated interest in license PPL 2A25.
Newton
The non-operated Newton assets field contributed 1,593 bopd of crude and condensate production to average daily working interest production
in 3Q 2026 (2Q 2026: 1,671 bopd), a decline of 5% QoQ while average daily working interest production in 9M 2026 was 1,547 bopd, marginal
decline of 1% YoY compared to 9M 2025: 1,566 bopd.
Drilling activities
In 3Q 2026 we completed four additional wells, Sibiri-3, Sibiri-4, OHS 1PAS-01 and Oben-60, bringing the total number of wells completed under
the 2026 onshore drilling programme to eight. As noted above, the Company successfully discovered hydrocarbons in the Aku-01 well.
We continue to actively manage well-specific execution challenges, with six rigs currently operating across our onshore portfolio to support
delivery of the remaining planned wells by year-end and sustain production growth.

In 3Q 2026 group average daily working interest gas production increased 7% QoQ to 219 MMscfd (2Q 2026: 204 MMscfd), as onshore
production growth fully offset the effects of the temporary suspension of gas production offshore. Average daily working interest gas production
from the Group's onshore assets increased by 21% to 208 MMscfd QoQ (2Q 2026: 171 MMscfd) driven by continued improvements in production
from the Western Assets and the ongoing ramp-up of ANOH, where production increased by 65% QoQ. On the offshore assets, average daily
working interest gas production was 11 MMscfd in 3Q 2026, down 67% QoQ (2Q 2026: 33 MMscfd), primarily as a result of the temporary
suspension of commercial gas sales during the quarter, due to accidental third-party damage to the Oso-BRT subsea gas pipeline. As a result
gas sales offshore were limited to contributions from OML 99, with gas production via Oso limted to volumes sufficient to power the NGL plant.
In 9M 2026, the Group produced 53.2 Bscf of gas, equivalent to average daily working interest production of 194.9 MMscfd, a YoY growth of 12%
compared with 9M 2025 (47.7 Bscf, equivalent to average daily working interest production of 174.7 MMscfd). The growth reflects the contribution
of ANOH following its first gas in January 2026.
Oben Gas Plant
Following the gas supply and revenue-sharing agreement signed with Gwato Energy LLP in 2Q 2026, activities progressed during the quarter to
advance the project towards delivery. Front-End Engineering Design (FEED) for the pipeline connection to the Oben Gas Plant reached 45%
completion. Activities within Gwato Energy’s scope continue to progress against its current project schedule, with Seplat providing technical
support where required.
ANOH Gas Processing Company (AGPC)
The ANOH gas plant continued to increase processing throughput, achieving a gross daily average output of 164 MMScfd in 3Q 2026, and is
currently operating at 70-80% of nameplate capacity. Of note, a successful test of plant nameplate throughput was achieved during the quarter,
supporting continued growth in the future. The majority of processed gas was sold to NLNG and Indorama. In addition, NGIC completed and
commissioned the OB3 pipeline during the quarter and we are pleased to report that commercial flow of gas from ANOH via OB3 commenced
on 1 September, with approximately 10% of 3Q 2026 gas sales via the OB3 pipeline.
Following the commencement of condensate exports through the Trans Niger Pipeline (TNP) to Bonny Terminal, on 14 July, AGPC completed its
maiden condensate lifting (641 kbbls). A second condensate lifting was completed at the end of August (551 kbbls) and a third is planned for early
4Q (1,190 kbbls). In addition, smaller condensate volumes continue to be loaded directly from the plant for the domestic market. As previously
disclosed, ANOH currently has 12.5 kbopd reserved production capacity (RPC) in the TNP, and a further ~1 kbopd of contracted offtake off-the-
fence.
During the quarter, AGPC made its first principal repayments on its debt facilities, a total of $68million paid to lenders during the period.
Oso - BRT Phase 1
The Oso-BRT pipeline upgrade project, designed to increase gas supply capacity to NLNG from 120 MMscfd to 240 MMscfd, remains on track for
completion in 4Q 2026. Key engineering and construction activities progressed during the quarter, including mobilisation for the BRT scope and
preparatory works ahead of final execution. Production contribution is expected in early 2027 once the sectional replacement of the Oso-BRT
pipeline has been completed.
There were no gas sales from Oso during the period with gas production limited to rates sufficient only to power the Bonny River Terminal, given
the damage previously incurred. Remediation of the previously reported Oso-BRT pipeline damage is progressing in parallel with the upgrade
project. As an interim measure, we have engaged a CNG power provider to support operations at BRT while the sectional replacement for the
damaged 12” gas pipeline is underway. This temporary solution enables continued NGL production and helps mitigate the operational impact of
the pipeline outage.

Financial review
The global crude oil market remained relatively tight during the period, with supply disruptions and geopolitical tensions across the Middle East
and Europe supporting oil prices. Brent crude averaged $88.07/bbl in the first nine months of the year, up 26% from $69.73/bbl the equivalent
period in 2025. Nigerian crude continued to attract strong demand, benefiting from its high quality and lower exposure to key global shipping and
supply chain bottlenecks, on top of which our simple hedge strategy and lifting timing meant that our average realised crude oil price increased
by 34% to $96.19/bbl (9M 2025: $71.93/bbl), an average premium of $8.12/bbl to Brent ($2.20/bbl in 9M 2025), representing an 11% premium to
the average Brent price year to date. We also benefited from positive pricing momentum across our gas business, with average realised NGL
prices increasing by 4% to $48.48/bbl supported by a more favourable product mix and a higher contribution from pentane, while average
realised dry gas prices increased by 8% to $3.18/mscf.
Overall, the combination of higher realised prices, increased production, and continued cost discipline provided a strong foundation for financial
performance during the period. Together with a more favourable fiscal profile, these factors supported robust growth in earnings and cash
generation.

2026 from $14.1/boe in 9M 2025 and above the Group's guidance range. The business continued to benefit from the completion of the End of
Routine Flaring (EORF) projects, with gas flare fees declining 40% to $21.7 million (9M 2025: $36.0 million).
Non-production costs increased primarily due to higher royalty expenses and DD&A charges. Royalty expense increased 17% YOY reflecting the
stronger commodity price environment with unit royalty increasing to $11.3/boe (9M 2025: $10.3/boe), partially offset by the fiscal benefits arising
from the PIA conversion of the Group's onshore assets. Higher DD&A also reflected the unusually low 9M 2025 comparator following the
adoption of a revised unit-of-production depreciation rate after completion of the Offshore Asset Competent Person's Report (CPR).

stronger share price performance during the period and the requirement to satisfy associated long-term incentive awards. As a result, G&A
expenses on a unit basis were $5.0/boe in 9M 2026 (9M 2025: $4.6/boe), at the upper end of the 2026 guidance range of $4.5/boe to $5.0/boe.
The other income/(loss) line is primarily made up of Underlift of $40.1 million, unrealized and realized FX gains of $11.3 million and $1.0 million
respectively, and other tariffs from the usage of the group’s pipeline by other entities and crude marketing income.
After adjusting the reported EBITDA of $1,533.9 million for non-cash items, the adjusted EBITDA for the period was $1,529.9 million (9M 2025:
$1,111.9 million), resulting in a margin of 55% (9M 2025: 51%).
Taxation
The income tax expense of $722.4 million (9M 2025: $475.0 million) includes a current tax charge of $729.2 million (9M 2025: $474.3 million) partly
offset by a deferred tax credit of $6.8 million (9M 2025: $0.6 million). This reflects an effective tax rate (‘ETR’) of 74%, lower than the 83% reported
in 9M 2025. The reduction in ETR is largely driven by the conversion of our onshore assets (excluding Elcrest) to the fiscal terms of the Petroleum
Industry Act (PIA), under which the applicable tax rate is 60%, compared to 85% under the Petroleum Profits Tax (PPT). In addition, deferred tax
recoverability has been recognised at this lower 60% rate, further contributing to the reduction in ETR.
The 9M 2026 tax numbers were computed based on the 2026 full-year ETR estimate in line with IAS 34. Cash taxes paid year-to-date totalled
$501.9 million, representing 34% of pre-tax cash flows from operations.

Cash call collection from our JV partners remained positive in the period. In 9M 2026, on the NEPL/Seplat JV for Western Assets and Elcrest, we
received $244 million in cash calls from our JV partner, lowering the receivables balance on the JV to $61.6 million (FY 2025: $84.5 million).
For our NUIMS/Seplat JV and SEPNU/NNPC JV for our Eastern Assets and SEPNU, we received $921 million in cash call settlement in 9M 2026,
bringing outstanding cash call receivables on both JVs to $105.6 million (FY 2025: $201.7 million). Payment under the previously agreed 18-month
settlement arrangement with NUIMS for $188 million of $325 million outstanding legacy cash call receivables commenced in September with a
further payment received post period-end in October. We continue to audit and seek reconciliation of the remaining balance with our JV partner.

Debt Movements
Advanced payment facility (‘APF’)
As previously reported, during 2Q 2026 the Company repaid and cancelled $200 million of the advance payment facility (APF) used to support
the MPNU acquisition, reducing the outstanding drawn balance to $100 million at the end of 3Q 2026.
Subsequent to the period end, on 27 October, the outstanding $100 million balance was repaid and the facility fully cancelled. As a result, the
Company has fully repaid all acquisition debt associated with the acquisition of MPNU in December 2024.
Credit ratings
Seplat maintains corporate credit ratings with Moody's Ratings, Standard & Poor's Rating Services (S&P) and Fitch Ratings (Fitch). The current
corporate ratings are as follows: (i) Moody's B2 (positive); (ii) S&P B+ (stable); (iii) Fitch B (stable).
In September 2026, Moody’s revised Seplat’s outlook to Positive from Stable, while affirming its B2 long-term corporate family rating, following a
similar revision to positive from stable for the outlook on the Government of Nigeria, while affirming Nigeria’s B3 local- and foreign-currency issuer
ratings. This followed S&P’s upgrade of Seplat’s long-term issuer credit rating to B+ (Stable) from B (Positive) in May 2026, following its upgrade of
Nigeria’s sovereign rating. In March 2026, Fitch affirmed Seplat’s Long-Term Issuer Default Rating at B with a Stable outlook.

The Group remains focused on maintaining a prudent capital structure, with a corporate policy of keeping Net Debt-to-EBITDA below 1.5x,
compared with a debt covenant of 3.0x. At the end of September 2026, Net Debt-to-EBITDA improved to 0.14x, from 0.53x at year-end 2025,
remaining comfortably within the Group's leverage policy and covenant threshold.
Dividend
The Board has approved a 3Q 2026 dividend of USD 19.0 cents per share, subject to applicable withholding tax, comprising a USD 5.0 cents per
share base dividend and a USD 14.0 cents per share special dividend. This represents a 153% increase compared with the total dividend declared
for 3Q 2025 (USD 7.5 cents per share) and reflects the Group’s strong financial position and supportive commodity price environment.
The Company remains on track with its previously communicated plan to distribute USD 45.0 cents per share, approximately $270 million, for the
2026 financial year, with USD 40.0 cents per share declared year to date. This amount excludes any potential additional transaction-related
dividend associated with the proposed 10% working interest sale in the NNPCL/SEPNU joint venture to NNPC Limited.

Hedging
Seplat Energy’s hedging policy is designed to provide a degree of cash flow protection during periods of oil price weakness and volatility.
During 3Q 2026, we commenced our 2027 hedging programme, purchasing put options covering 6.0 MMbbls at a weighted average strike price
of $55.00/bbl and a weighted average premium of $0.77/bbl. We will continue to monitor oil market conditions and evaluate further hedging
opportunities in line with our policy. Our strategy of using simple put options, which provides downside price protection while retaining exposure
to higher oil prices, remains unchanged.
Post period end, we purchased additional put options covering 2.0 MMbbls at a strike price of $60.00/bbl and a premium of $0.96/bbl.

Interim sustainability disclosure for 9
months ended 30 September 2026
Introduction
This interim report provides an update on Seplat Energy’s sustainability-related programmes and activities for the nine months ended 30
September 2026. It provides additional context, refreshed metrics and recent developments since the publication of the 2025 Integrated Annual
Report.
The disclosures highlight material developments and changes during the third quarter of 2026. They should be read together with the
sustainability-related financial disclosures in the 2025 Integrated Annual Report, and the interim sustainability-related disclosures for the six
months ended 30 June 2026.
Previously reported disclosures on governance, strategy, risk management, reporting policies and methodologies have not been repeated where
there have been no material changes. Unless otherwise stated, the reporting boundary and measurement approaches remain consistent with
those applied in the 2025 Integrated Annual Report, and comparative information is prepared on a consistent basis and presented where
available and useful to users of general purpose financial reports.
Governance
The Group's governance structure for sustainability-related risks and opportunities remain as described in the 2025 Integrated Annual Report,
pages 112–113, which can be accessed here , Key activities are detailed below.

Qualitative
Metric Boundary 3Q 2026 Updates
During 3Q 2026, Seplat sustained its ISO 45001:2018 Occupational Health & Safety
Health, Safety and Security Onshore
Management System certification through the surveillance audit programme.
Water management progressed during 3Q 2026, with closure of identified onshore
Water & Wastewater reporting gaps increasing from 35% to 45% from Onshore water audit. The Offshore Water
Group
management Footprint Audit was completed, with corrective actions progressing to address identified
gaps and strengthen reporting accuracy.
Management systems
In 3Q 2026, Seplat onshore continued strengthening its ISO 14001 Environmental
Group Management System through certification close-out activities, management system
improvements, internal audits, management reviews and readiness initiatives supporting
ongoing certification objectives. Preparatory activities also commenced for the future
transition to the revised ISO 14001 standard for offshore.
Tree4life Afforestation
A total of 155,484 additional trees were planted during the period, bringing the cumulative
Ecological Impact Group number of trees planted in 2026 to 205,484 and the overall total since programme
inception to 436,512 trees. Furthermore, 20,000 seedlings were distributed to host
communities, increasing cumulative seedling distribution to 43,000 seedlings between 2024
and 2026.
Biodiversity Assessment of Offshore Operational areas and development of a
consolidated BAP for Seplat Group.
Offshore During 3Q 2026, the gap analysis and field data gathering for the Offshore Biodiversity
Assessment were completed, marking progress towards development of the consolidated
Biodiversity Action Plan (BAP). Report development is underway, with completion expected
by the end of 4Q 2026.
Assurance
External assurance has not been performed on the interim sustainability disclosure for 9 months ended 30 September 2026. The disclosures
have, however, been subject to the Group’s established managements review.
As part of its year-end reporting process, Seplat intends to obtain independent limited assurance over relevant FY2026 sustainability-related
disclosures, in accordance with ISSA 5000 ( International Standard on Sustainability Assurance) and the FRC Nigeria sustainability reporting
requirements. The final assurance scope, criteria and related details will be communicated in the 2026 Annual Report.

Outlook
Production guidance
2026 production guidance narrowed to the upper half of guidance, 145-155 kboepd.
Seplat Energy's production operations improved materially in 3Q 2026, bringing overall production to the mid-point of our prior guidance range. In
4Q 2026 we expect continued growth, driven by contribution from the ongoing idle well restoration programme, the onshore drilling programme
and continued ANOH ramp-up. This supports narrowing guidance to the upper half of the original guidance range (previously: 135-155 kboepd).
Our guidance excludes the effects of the sale of 10% in the offshore assets to our JV partner, further updates will be provided at completion.
Capex guidance
Working interest capital expenditure guidance is reduced to $280 million - $340 million.
9M 2026 cash capital investment was lower than planned, largely due to timing on certain projects costs and particularly due to the delayed start
of the offshore drilling programme. While cash capex is expected to increase in 4Q 2026, overall investment for 2026 is expected to be lower
than previous expectations (previously: $360-$440 million). Onshore drilling is expected to continue at a high tempo for the remainder of the year
and plans are underway for an active 2027 offshore drilling campaign.
Opex guidance
Unit operating cost guidance for the Company raised into the range of $15.5-16.5/boe.
9M 2026 unit operating cost was above plan primarily due to the additional costs incurred at Yoho (equivalent to approximately $1.8/boe in 9M
2026). Without this additional cost, unit production opex would be within the original guidance range. Full year additional net costs on Yoho are
now expected to impact production opex by approximately $2.0/boe, as such we increase FY26 unit production opex guidance to $15.5-16.5/
boe.
We note that we are targeting recovery of a portion of Yoho costs through our insurance. Guidance on this potential recovery will be provided
when the outcome of the process is clear.
Other Financial and Strategic guidance
G&A: Unit G&A costs in 9M 2026 were $5.0/boe, within 2026 guidance of $4.5-5.0/boe, 2026 guidance is reiterated.
Tax: Given the continued elevated price environment, we update our FY2026 cash tax guidance. Assuming 2026 average realised oil price of
$95/bbl, we expect FY 2026 cash taxes in the range of $650-700 million (previously $600-650 million at $85/bbl).
Dividend: Our previously communicated guidance for the 2026 underlying business performance dividend of $270 million, equivalent to USD 45.0
cents/share, is retained. Of this USD 40.0 cents/share has been declared for 1Q, 2Q and 3Q combined.
In addition, the Company intends to distribute $140 million (USD 23.3 cents/share) as a Transaction dividend following the completion of the sale
of a 10% interest in the NNPCL/SEPNU Joint Venture to our JV partner. As a result, planned 2026 dividends to shareholders are expected to total
$410.0 million (or USD 68.3 c/shr).
Over the 2026-2030 5 year period the Company has committed to return at least $1.0 billion to shareholders via dividends. The plan targets
distributions of 40-50% of free cash flow over the five year cycle (2026-2030).
SEPNU Divestment
The previously announced sale of a 10% working interest in the assets held within the NNPCL/SEPNU Joint Venture to our joint venture partner is
progressing, and is expected to complete in 4Q 2026.
Note: Our 2026 guidance listed above exclude any adjustments that may be required due to the sale of the 10% interest in the NNPCL/SEPNU JV. Any changes will be communicated post-
completion of the transaction.

Seplat Energy Plc 28 9M 2026 Financial Results

In December 2024, the IASB issued Amendments to IFRS 9 and IFRS
7 - Contracts Referencing Nature dependent Electricity. The
amendments apply only to contracts that reference nature-
dependent electricity, and they:
• Clarify the application of the ‘own-use’ requirements for in-scope
contracts
• Amend the designation requirements for a hedged item in a cash
flow hedging relationship for in-scope contracts
• Add new disclosure requirements to enable investors to
understand the effect of these contracts on a company’s financial
performance and cash flows
The amendments had no impact on Group’s interim condensed
financial statements.

VII. Income taxes or asset life assumptions could result in a material adjustment to
inventory values in future reporting periods.
The Group is subject to income taxes by the Nigerian tax authority,
which does not require significant judgement in terms of provision for
income taxes, but a certain level of judgement is required for
recognition of deferred tax assets. Management is required to assess
the ability of the Group to generate future taxable economic earnings
that will be used to recover all deferred tax assets. Assumptions about
the generation of future taxable profits depend on management’s
estimates of future cash flows. The estimates are based on the future
cash flow from operations taking into consideration the oil and gas
prices, volumes produced, operational and capital expenditure.
VIII. Impairment of financial assets
The loss allowances for financial assets are based on assumptions
about risk of default, expected loss rates and maximum contractual
period. The Group uses judgement in making these assumptions and
selecting the inputs to the impairment calculation, based on the
Group’s past history, existing market conditions as well as forward
looking estimates at the end of each reporting period. Details of the
key assumptions and inputs used are disclosed in note 5.1.1.
IX. Intangible assets
The contract based intangible assets (licence) were acquired as part
of a business combination. They are recognised at their fair value at
the date of acquisition and are subsequently amortised on a straight-
line basis over their estimated remaining useful lives of the asset. The
fair value of contract based intangible assets is estimated using the
multi period excess earnings method. This requires a forecast of
revenue and all cost projections throughout the useful life of the
intangible assets. A contributory asset charge that reflects the return
on assets is also determined and applied to the revenue but

5. Financial risk management
5.1 Financial risk factors
The Group’s activities expose it to a variety of financial risks such as market risk (including foreign exchange risk, interest rate risk and commodity
price risk), credit risk and liquidity risk. The Group’s risk management programme focuses on the unpredictability of financial markets and seeks to
minimise potential adverse effects on the Group’s financial performance. The management of financial risks is carried out by the corporate
finance team under policies approved by the Board of Directors. The Board provides written principles for overall risk management, as well as
written policies covering specific areas, such as foreign exchange risk, interest rate risk, credit risk and investment of excess liquidity
Risk Exposure arising from Measurement Management

5.1.1 Credit risk
Credit risk refers to the risk of a counterparty defaulting on its contractual obligations resulting in financial loss to the Group. Credit risk arises from
investments, cash and bank balances as well as credit exposures to customers (i.e. MSN Energy, Transcorp, Shell western, Pillar, Azura, Geregu
Power, Sapele Power, ExxonMobil and Nigerian Gas Marketing Company (NGMC) receivables), and other parties (i.e., NUIMS JV receivables, NEPL
JV receivables and other receivables)
a) Risk management
The Group is exposed to credit risk from its sale of crude oil to ExxonMobil, Waltersmith, Chevron, Vitol S.A and Shell western. The Group has in
place a 30-day payment term bill of lading date in the offtake agreement with Shell Western Supply and Trading Limited. The Group is exposed
to further credit risk from outstanding cash calls from NEPL and NUIMS.
In addition, the Group is exposed to credit risk in relation to the sale of gas to its customers.
The credit risk on cash and bank balances is managed through the diversification of banks in which the balances are held. The risk is limited
because the majority of deposits are with banks that have an acceptable credit rating assigned by an international credit agency. The Group’s
maximum exposure to credit risk due to default of the counterparty is equal to the carrying value of its financial assets.
b) Estimation uncertainty in measuring impairment loss

estimates used in calculating impairment losses on those financial assets at the end of the reporting period. These methods, assumptions and
estimates have a significant risk of causing material adjustments to the carrying amounts of the Group’s financial assets.

5.1.2 Liquidity risk
Liquidity risk is the risk that the Group will not be able to meet its financial obligations as they fall due. The Group manages liquidity risk by ensuring
that sufficient funds are available to meet its commitments as they fall due.
The Group uses both long-term and short-term cash flow projections to monitor funding requirements for activities and to ensure there are
sufficient cash resources to meet operational needs. Cash flow projections take into consideration the Group’s debt financing plans and
covenant compliance. Surplus cash held is transferred to the treasury department which invests in interest bearing current accounts and time
deposits.

5.1.4 Fair Value Hierarchy
As at the reporting period, the Group had classified its financial instruments into the three levels prescribed under the accounting standards.
There were no transfers of financial instruments between fair value hierarchy levels during the period.
• Level 1 – Quoted (unadjusted) market prices in active markets for identical assets or liabilities.
• Level 2 – Valuation techniques for which the lowest level input that is significant to the fair value measurement is directly or indirectly
observable.
• Level 3 – Valuation techniques for which the lowest level input that is significant to the fair value measurement is unobservable.
The fair value of the financial instruments is included at the price that would be received to sell an asset or paid to transfer a liability in an orderly
transaction between market participants at the measurement date.
The fair value of the Group’s derivative financial instruments has been determined using a proprietary pricing model that uses marked to market
valuation. The valuation represents the mid-market value and the actual close-out costs of trades involved. The market inputs to the model are
derived from observable sources. Other inputs are unobservable but are estimated based on the market inputs or by using other pricing models.
The derivative financial instruments are in level 2.
The fair value of the Group’s interest-bearing loans and borrowings is determined by using discounted cash flow models that use market interest
rates as at the end of the period. The interest-bearing loans and borrowings are in level 2.
The fair value of other financial assets at amortised cost is determined by reference to quoted prices in an active market, i.e FMDQ. Accordingly,
these other financial assets at amortised cost are classified within Level 1 of the fair value hierarchy.

6. Segment reporting
Business segments are based on the Group’s internal organisation and management reporting structure. The Group’s business segments are the
two core businesses: Oil and Gas. The Oil segment deals with the exploration, development and production of crude oil while the Gas segment
deals with the production and processing of gas. These two reportable segments make up the total operations of the Group.
For the nine months ended 30 September 2026, revenue from the gas segment of the business constituted 10% (9M 2025: 8%) of the Group’s
revenue and about 62% (9M 2025: 45%) of the Group’s profit - mostly due to positive fiscal regime associated with the gas business.
Management is committed to continued growth of the gas segment and will procure the required infrastructure for this segment of the business.
The gas business is positioned separately within the Group and reports directly to the Director New Energy and Gas (chief operating decision
maker). As the gas business segment’s revenues, results and cash flows are largely independent of other business units within the Group, it is
regarded as a separate segment. The result is two reporting segments, Oil and Gas. There were no inter segment sales during the reporting
periods under consideration, therefore all revenue was from external customers.
Amounts relating to the gas segment are determined using the gas cost centres, with the exception of depreciation. Depreciation relating to the
gas segment is determined by applying a percentage which reflects the proportion of the Net Book Value of oil and gas properties that relates to
gas investment costs (i.e., cost for the gas processing and other gas facilities).
The Group accounting policies are also applied in the segment reports.

Operational & maintenance expenses relate mainly to maintenance costs, warehouse operations expenses, security expenses, community
expenses, clean-up costs, fuel supplies and catering services. The increase in operational and maintenance expense in 9M 2026 is principally
driven by Yoho restoration expense of ₦92 billion. Included in operational and maintenance expenses is gas flare penalty of ₦29.7 billion (9M
2025: ₦55.5 billion) reflecting the early gains on completion of end of routine flaring projects in the onshore business.
Barging and Trucking costs relate to costs on the OML 40 Gbetiokun field.

Underlifts and overlifts represent differences between crude oil lifted and the Group's entitlement based on its share of production. Underlifts are
measured at current market value. Overlifts are measured by reference to the observable lifted price at the date of lifting. Movements during the
period are recognised within other income/(loss).
Gain on foreign exchange is principally due to the translation of Naira, Pounds and Euro denominated monetary assets and liabilities.
Others include tariffs from the usage of the Group’s pipeline by others.

In accordance with IAS 34: Interim Financial Reporting, income tax expense for the interim period has been determined using the estimated
weighted average annual effective income tax rate expected for the full financial year. This estimate is reviewed at each interim reporting date
and adjusted, where necessary, to reflect changes in the expected annual tax rate.
Income tax expense for the Group is analysed by Oil segment - 9 months ended ₦929.9 billion (9M 2025: ₦695.9 billion); 3 months ended
₦396.2 billion (3M 2025: ₦342.4 billion) and Gas Segment - 9 months ended ₦57.2 billion (9M 2025: ₦36.4 billion); 3 months ended ₦26.0 billion
(3M 2025: ₦21.6 billion).
The Group has applied the provisions of the Nigeria Tax Act 2025 which became effective in January 2026 and require Education Tax, the NASENI
Levy, and the Police Trust Fund Levy to be consolidated into Development Levy.

A contract asset is an entity’s right to consideration in exchange for goods or services that the entity has transferred to a customer. The Group
has recognised an asset in relation to a contract with Sapele Power, Azura, NGMC, Transcorp Power, MSN Energy, Egbin Power, AGPC,
Waltersmith, Shell Western and Chevron, for the delivery of oil and gas supplies which these customers have received but which has not been
invoiced as at the end of the reporting period.
The terms of payment relating to the contract is between 30- 45 days from the invoice date. However, invoices are raised after delivery between
14-21 days when the receivable amount has been established and the right to the receivables crystallises. The right to the unbilled receivables is
recognised as a contract asset. Upon obtaining gas receipt certificates, bill of lading and other reconciliation documents with the respective
customers/offtakers, after which invoices are issued, the respective balances are reclassified from contract assets to trade receivables

19. Derivative financial instruments
The Group uses its derivatives for economic hedging purposes and not as speculative investments. Derivatives are measured at fair value
through profit or loss. Derivative financial assets and liabilities are presented as current to the extent they are expected to be realised or settled
within 12 months after the reporting period.
The fair value has been determined using a proprietary pricing model which generates results from inputs. The market inputs to the model are
derived from observable sources. Other inputs are unobservable but are estimated based on the market inputs or by using other pricing models.

$650 million Senior notes
On 21 March 2025, the Group refinanced the $650m notes due 2026 with a new $650m issuance maturing in 2030. The newly issued $650m
notes due in 2030 carry a coupon rate of 9.125%, reflecting prevailing global market volatility at the time of issuance. The $650 million bond
issuance was used exclusively to redeem the maturing $650 million note, with transaction costs covered from the Group’s cash reserves.The
amortised cost for the senior notes as at the reporting period is ₦847.04 billion, $637.52 million (Dec 2025: ₦932.5 billion, $649.7 million) although
the principal is $650 million.
$80 million Senior reserve-based lending (RBL) facility - refinanced facility
On 30 September 2025, the Group, through its subsidiary Westport, successfully completed the refinancing of the Westport RBL Facility. The new
facility is an $80M RBL facility with Standard Bank (USD 35 million), Mauritius Commercial Bank (USD 25 million), First City Monument Bank (USD 12
million), and Zenith Bank (USD 8 million). Zenith Bank represents a new participant in this financing. The refinancing resulted in improved pricing
terms, with the facility now carrying a margin of 6.5% for the first three years, increasing to 7.0% from year four onwards should the facility remain
drawn.
These margins are more favourable than those under the previous senior (8.0% plus a CAS of 0.25%) and junior (10.5% plus a CAS of 0.25%)
facilities. The final maturity date is five years from the effective date, with an 18-month moratorium on principal repayments. Current drawdown
under the financing is $55.25 million, which is below the committed level of $80 million for the period up to and including 30 June 2027. The
amortised cost for the senior notes as at the reporting period is ₦73.31 billion, $55.18 million (Dec 2025:₦76.7 billion, $53.6 million).
$400 million Revolving credit facility
The RCF was refinanced to $400 million on January 31, 2026. All in pricing improved to SOFR + 4.5% from SOFR + 5.26% (including a credit
adjustment spread). The facility is no longer amortising and includes a bullet repayment at final maturity, with maturity extended to 30 September
2029, i.e., maturing before the $650 million senior notes. The facility is currently fully undrawn.
$300 million Advance payment facility
The $300 million APF was closed in December 2024 to provide financing for the completion of the MPNU acquisition. The facility was fully drawn;
however, the Group has repaid and cancelled $200M under the APF in May and June 2026. The current outstanding drawdown amount is $100
million.
The APF bears interest at a rate of the aggregate of Term SOFR (including a credit adjustment spread of 0.25% per annum) plus 5% per annum
and matures in December 2027. The amortised cost for the APF as at the reporting period is ₦131.61 billion, $99.05 million (Dec 2025: ₦433.9
billion, $302.3 million).

25.1. Share-based payment liability
The Group granted share-based awards to employees that will be settled in cash. These awards comprise the 2024 LTIP, 2025 LTIP and 2026
LTIP. Awards granted under these LTIPs to employees below Board level will be settled in cash and have a three year vesting period. Share-
based payment liability represents the Group’s obligation in respect of these cash-settled LTIP awards granted to employees below Board level.

awards.. Following the modification, a cash-settled share-based payment liability was recognised in accordance with IFRS 2. The modification
resulted in a transfer of ₦5.94 billion from share-based payment reserve to share-based payment liability and an incremental fair value expense
of ₦31.29 billion, which has been recognised as share-based payment expense during the reporting period. The fair value of the share-based
liability was subsequently remeasured at 30 September 2026 using a binomial valuation model.

26. Earnings per share EPS
Basic
Basic EPS is calculated on the Group’s profit after taxation attributable to the parent entity, which is based on the weighted average number of
issued and fully paid ordinary shares during the period.
Diluted
Diluted EPS is calculated by dividing the profit after taxation attributable to the parent entity by the weighted average number of ordinary shares
outstanding during the period plus all the dilutive potential ordinary shares (arising from outstanding share awards in the share-based payment
scheme) into ordinary shares.

27. Proposed dividend
For the third quarter ended 30 September 2026, the Group’s directors declared an interim dividend of ₦252.45 per share, consisting of core
dividend of ₦66.43 per share and special dividend of ₦186.01 per share. (Dec 2025: final dividend of ₦72.15 and special dividend of ₦47.62).
28. Related party relationships and transactions
The Group is controlled by Seplat Energy Plc (the parent Company).
During the period, Heirs Energies and Heirs Holding Limited acquired 21.07% of the issued share capital of the Seplat Energy PLC. These entities
are controlled by Mr Tony O Elumelu CFR. He was appointed to the Board effective 22 January 2026.
The following are details of transactions and balances with related parties. The terms of these transactions are at arm’s length.
Entities controlled by Directors of the Group
United Bank for Africa: Mr. Tony Elumelu is a Director and shareholder of United Bank for Africa Plc (UBA). UBA provides the Group with joint
venture–related banking services, financing, and direct banking support. The bank is also one of the Group’s lenders under the ₦551.7 billion
revolving credit facility (‘RCF’), which remained undrawn as at the reporting date. UBA also provides a ₦1.3 billion customs bond for operational
cargo clearance for an annual fee of 0.15%. At the end of the reporting period, the Group held balances of ₦119.7 billion with UBA, comprising joint
venture and corporate balances.
Transcorp Power Limited: During the period, the Group engaged in gas sales transactions with Transcorp Power Limited, an entity controlled by
a Director of the Group (Mr. Tony Elumelu), amounting to ₦3.1 billion. As at the reporting date, amounts due from Transcorp Power Limited was
₦8.2 billion.
Transcorp Hotel Plc: Transcorp Hotels Plc is an entity controlled by a Director of the Group (Mr. Tony Elumelu). During the period, Transcorp
Hotels Plc provided hospitality services to the Group, with services rendered amounting to ₦1.1 billion. The Group had an outstanding payable
balance of ₦30.1 million with Transcorp Hotels Plc as at the reporting date.
Shareholders of the parent company
Vaaris Resources JVCo Limited: Following the sale of TotalEnergies EP Nigeria Limited’s (TEPNG) non-operated interest in the Renaissance JV
(formerly SPDC) licences to Vaaris, Seplat consented, through a Deed of Novation, to Vaaris replacing TEPNG under the ANOH Unitization and
Unit Operating Agreement (UUOA), to which the Renaissance JV is a party. Vaaris is controlled by Platform Petroleum, a shareholder of Seplat. No
transactions or outstanding balances arose from the arrangement.
Entities controlled by Directors of the Company
Vaaris Resources JVCo Limited: Mr Kazeem Raimi, a Non-Executive Director of Seplat PLC, is also a director of Vaaris, which replaced TEPNG
under the ANOH Unitization and Unit Operating Agreement (UUOA) pursuant to a Deed of Novation consented to by Seplat during the period. No
transactions or outstanding balances arose from the arrangement
29. Commitments and contingencies
29.1 Contingent liabilities
The Group is involved in a number of legal suits as defendant. The estimated value of the contingent liabilities for the period ended 30 Sept 2026
is ₦450.17 billion, (Dec 2025: ₦395.77 billion). The contingent liability for the year is determined based on possible occurrences, though unlikely to
occur. No provision has been made for this potential liability in these financial statements. Management and the Group’s solicitors are of the
opinion that the Group will suffer no loss from these claims.
30. Events after the reporting period
There are no other events which could have had a material effect on the financial position of the Group as at 30 September 2026 and its financial
performance for the period then ended that have not been adequately provided for or disclosed in these financial statements.

Seplat Energy Plc 81 9M 2026 Financial Results

c) Contracts Referencing Nature-dependent
Electricity – Amendments to IFRS 9 and IFRS 7
In December 2024, the IASB issued Amendments to IFRS 9 and IFRS
7 - Contracts Referencing Nature dependent Electricity. The
amendments apply only to contracts that reference nature-
dependent electricity, and they:
• Clarify the application of the ‘own-use’ requirements for in-scope
contracts
• Amend the designation requirements for a hedged item in a cash
flow hedging relationship for in-scope contracts
• Add new disclosure requirements to enable investors to
understand the effect of these contracts on a company’s financial
performance and cash flows
The amendments had no impact on Group’s interim condensed
financial statements.

the generation of future taxable profits depend on management’s

cash flow from operations taking into consideration the oil and gas
prices, volumes produced, operational and capital expenditure.
VIII. Impairment of financial assets
The loss allowances for financial assets are based on assumptions
about risk of default, expected loss rates and maximum contractual
period. The Group uses judgement in making these assumptions and
selecting the inputs to the impairment calculation, based on the
Group’s past history, existing market conditions as well as forward
looking estimates at the end of each reporting period. Details of the
key assumptions and inputs used are disclosed in note 5.1.1.

of a business combination. They are recognised at their fair value at

5. Financial risk management
5.1 Financial risk factors
The Group’s activities expose it to a variety of financial risks such as market risk (including foreign exchange risk, interest rate risk and commodity
price risk), credit risk and liquidity risk. The Group’s risk management programme focuses on the unpredictability of financial markets and seeks to
minimise potential adverse effects on the Group’s financial performance. The management of financial risks is carried out by the corporate
finance team under policies approved by the Board of Directors. The Board provides written principles for overall risk management, as well as
written policies covering specific areas, such as foreign exchange risk, interest rate risk, credit risk and investment of excess liquidity

5.1.1 Credit risk
Credit risk refers to the risk of a counterparty defaulting on its contractual obligations resulting in financial loss to the Group. Credit risk arises from
investments, cash and bank balances as well as credit exposures to customers (i.e. MSN Energy, Transcorp, Shell western, Pillar, Azura, Geregu
Power, Sapele Power, ExxonMobil and Nigerian Gas Marketing Company (NGMC) receivables), and other parties (i.e., NUIMS JV receivables, NEPL
JV receivables and other receivables)
a) Risk management
The Group is exposed to credit risk from its sale of crude oil to ExxonMobil, Waltersmith, Chevron, Vitol S.A and Shell western. The Group has in
place a 30-day payment term bill of lading date in the offtake agreement with Shell Western Supply and Trading Limited. The Group is exposed
to further credit risk from outstanding cash calls from NEPL and NUIMS.
In addition, the Group is exposed to credit risk in relation to the sale of gas to its customers.
The credit risk on cash and bank balances is managed through the diversification of banks in which the balances are held. The risk is limited
because the majority of deposits are with banks that have an acceptable credit rating assigned by an international credit agency. The Group’s
maximum exposure to credit risk due to default of the counterparty is equal to the carrying value of its financial assets.
b) Estimation uncertainty in measuring impairment loss

5.1.2 Liquidity risk
Liquidity risk is the risk that the Group will not be able to meet its financial obligations as they fall due. The Group manages liquidity risk by ensuring
that sufficient funds are available to meet its commitments as they fall due.
The Group uses both long-term and short-term cash flow projections to monitor funding requirements for activities and to ensure there are
sufficient cash resources to meet operational needs. Cash flow projections take into consideration the Group’s debt financing plans and
covenant compliance. Surplus cash held is transferred to the treasury department which invests in interest-bearing current accounts and time
deposits.

5.1.4 Fair Value Hierarchy
As at the reporting period, the Group had classified its financial instruments into the three levels prescribed under the accounting standards.
There were no transfers of financial instruments between fair value hierarchy levels during the period.
• Level 1 – Quoted (unadjusted) market prices in active markets for identical assets or liabilities.
• Level 2 – Valuation techniques for which the lowest level input that is significant to the fair value measurement is directly or indirectly
observable.
• Level 3 – Valuation techniques for which the lowest level input that is significant to the fair value measurement is unobservable.
The fair value of the financial instruments is included at the price that would be received to sell an asset or paid to transfer a liability in an orderly
transaction between market participants at the measurement date.
The fair value of the Group’s derivative financial instruments has been determined using a proprietary pricing model that uses marked to market
valuation. The valuation represents the mid-market value and the actual close-out costs of trades involved. The market inputs to the model are
derived from observable sources. Other inputs are unobservable but are estimated based on the market inputs or by using other pricing models.
The derivative financial instruments are in level 2.
The fair value of the Group’s interest-bearing loans and borrowings is determined by using discounted cash flow models that use market interest
rates as at the end of the period. The interest-bearing loans and borrowings are in level 2.
The fair value of other financial assets at amortised cost is determined by reference to quoted prices in an active market, i.e FMDQ. Accordingly,
these other financial assets at amortised cost are classified within Level 1 of the fair value hierarchy.

6. Segment reporting
Business segments are based on the Group’s internal organisation and management reporting structure. The Group’s business segments are the
two core businesses: Oil and Gas. The Oil segment deals with the exploration, development and production of crude oil while the Gas segment
deals with the production and processing of gas. These two reportable segments make up the total operations of the Group.
For the nine months ended 30 September 2026, revenue from the gas segment of the business constituted 10% (2025: 8%) of the Group’s
revenue and about 61.9% of the Group’s profit - mostly due to positive fiscal regime associated with the gas business. Management is
committed to continued growth of the gas segment and will procure the required infrastructure for this segment of the business. The gas
business is positioned separately within the Group and reports directly to the Director New Energy and Gas (chief operating decision maker). As
the gas business segment’s revenues, results and cash flows are largely independent of other business units within the Group, it is regarded as a
separate segment. The result is two reporting segments, Oil and Gas. There were no inter segment sales during the reporting periods under
consideration, therefore all revenue was from external customers.
Amounts relating to the gas segment are determined using the gas cost centres, with the exception of depreciation. Depreciation relating to the
gas segment is determined by applying a percentage which reflects the proportion of the Net Book Value of oil and gas properties that relates to
gas investment costs (i.e., cost for the gas processing and other gas facilities).
The Group accounting policies are also applied in the segment reports.

Operational & maintenance expenses relate mainly to maintenance costs, warehouse operations expenses, security expenses, community
expenses, clean-up costs, fuel supplies and catering services. The increase in operational and maintenance expense in 9M 2026 is principally
driven by Yoho restoration expense of $67 million. Included in operational and maintenance expenses is gas flare penalty of $21.7 million (9M
2025: $36.0 million) reflecting the early gains on completion of end of routine flaring projects in the onshore business.
Barging and Trucking costs relate to costs on the OML 40 Gbetiokun field.
9. Other income/(loss) - net
9 months 9 months 3 Months 3 Months
ended ended ended ended
30 Sept 2026 30 Sept 2025 30 Sept 2026 30 Sept 2025
$'000 $'000 $'000 $'000
Underlift/(Overlift) 40,054 3,826 107,080 (38,765)

99,604 26,553 128,690 (24,454)
Underlifts and overlifts represent differences between crude oil lifted and the Group's entitlement based on its share of production. Underlifts are
measured at current market value. Overlifts are measured by reference to the observable lifted price at the date of lifting. Movements during the
period are recognised within other income/(loss).
Gain on foreign exchange is principally due to the translation of Naira, Pounds and Euro denominated monetary assets and liabilities.
Others include tariffs from the usage of the Group’s pipeline by others.

In accordance with IAS 34: Interim Financial Reporting, income tax expense for the interim period has been determined using the estimated
weighted average annual effective income tax rate expected for the full financial year. This estimate is reviewed at each interim reporting date
and adjusted, where necessary, to reflect changes in the expected annual tax rate.
Income tax expense for the Group is analysed by Oil segment - 9 months ended $680.5 million (9M 2025: $451.3 million); 3 months ended
$292.3 million (3M 2025: $223.3 million) and Gas Segment - 9 months ended $41.9 million (9M 2025: $23.6 million); 3 months ended $19.2 million
(3M 2025: ($13.8 million)).
The Group has applied the provisions of the Nigeria Tax Act 2025 which became effective in January 2026 and require Education Tax, the NASENI
Levy, and the Police Trust Fund Levy to be consolidated into Development Levy.

Impairment allowance on contract assets (2,800) (1,717)
38,937 20,315
A contract asset is an entity’s right to consideration in exchange for goods or services that the entity has transferred to a customer. The Group
has recognised an asset in relation to a contract with Sapele Power, Azura, NGMC, Transcorp Power, MSN Energy, Egbin Power, AGPC,
Waltersmith, Shell Western and Chevron, for the delivery of oil and gas supplies which these customers have received but which has not been
invoiced as at the end of the reporting period.
The terms of payment relating to the contract is between 30- 45 days from the invoice date. However, invoices are raised after delivery between
14-21 days when the receivable amount has been established and the right to the receivables crystallises. The right to the unbilled receivables is
recognised as a contract asset. Upon obtaining gas receipt certificates, bill of lading and other reconciliation documents with the respective
customers/offtakers, after which invoices are issued, the respective balances are reclassified from contract assets to trade receivables.

19. Derivative financial instruments
The Group uses its derivatives for economic hedging purposes and not as speculative investments. Derivatives are measured at fair value
through profit or loss. Derivative financial assets and liabilities are presented as current to the extent they are expected to be realised or settled
within 12 months after the reporting period.
The fair value has been determined using a proprietary pricing model which generates results from inputs. The market inputs to the model are
derived from observable sources. Other inputs are unobservable but are estimated based on the market inputs or by using other pricing models.

Included in the restricted cash is $120.9 million (Dec 2025: $119.5 million), which relates to SEPNU’s decommissioning and abandonment deposit of
$103.3 million(Dec 2025:$103.34 million) , as well as the host community fund of $17.6 million (Dec 2025: $16.2 million). SEPNU's decommissioning
and abandonment deposit has been classified as current because it is held under a one-year letter of credit facility.
Also included in the restricted cash balance is $2.8 million (Dec 2025: $2.4 million) and $4.4 million (Dec 2025: $3.4 million) set aside in the
stamping reserve account and debt service reserve account respectively for the revolving credit facility. The stamping reserve amount is to be
used for the settlement of all fees and costs payable for the purposes of stamping and registering the Security Documents at the stamp duties
office and at the Corporate Affairs Commission (CAC).
A garnishee order of $0.6 million (Dec 2025: $0.6 million) is included in the restricted cash balance as at the end of the reporting period.
Also included in the restricted cash balance is $0.5 million (Dec 2025: $0.4 million) for unclaimed dividend.
These amounts are subject to legal restrictions and are therefore not available for general use by the Group.

22.3 Employee share-based payment scheme
As at 30 Sept 2026, the Group had 29,575,922 shares (Dec 2025: 49,999,973 shares). comprising 6,918,200 equity-settled awards and
22,657,722 cash-settled awards, which were yet to fully vest. These shares have been assigned to certain employees and senior executives in
line with its share-based incentive scheme. During the nine months ended 30 Sept 2026, 22,229,727 shares were vested (Dec 2025: 13,342,715
shares).
22.4 Treasury shares
This relates to shares purchased from the market or issued by the company to fund the Group’s Long-Term Incentive Plan. The programme
commenced from 1 March 2021 and are held by the Trustees under the Trust for the benefit of the Group’s employee beneficiaries covered under
the Trust.

$650 million Senior notes
On 21 March 2025, the Group refinanced the $650m notes due 2026 with a new $650m issuance maturing in 2030. The newly issued $650m
notes due in 2030 carry a coupon rate of 9.125%, reflecting prevailing global market volatility at the time of issuance. The $650 million bond
issuance was used exclusively to redeem the maturing $650 million note, with transaction costs covered from the Group’s cash reserves. The
amortised cost for the senior notes as at the reporting period is $637.52 million (Dec 2025: $649.7 million) although the principal is $650 million.
$80 million Senior reserve-based lending (RBL) facility – refinanced facility
On 30 September 2025, the Group, through its subsidiary Westport, successfully completed the refinancing of the Westport RBL Facility. The new
facility is an $80M RBL facility with Standard Bank (USD 35 million), Mauritius Commercial Bank (USD 25 million), First City Monument Bank (USD 12
million), and Zenith Bank (USD 8 million). Zenith Bank represents a new participant in this financing. The refinancing resulted in improved pricing
terms, with the facility now carrying a margin of 6.5% for the first three years, increasing to 7.0% from year four onwards should the facility remain
drawn.
These margins are more favourable than those under the previous senior (8.0% plus a CAS of 0.25%) and junior (10.5% plus a CAS of 0.25%)
facilities. The final maturity date is five years from the effective date, with an 18-month moratorium on principal repayments. Current drawdown
under the financing is $55.25 million, which is below the committed level of $80 million for the period up to and including 30 June 2027. The
amortised cost for the senior notes as at the reporting period is $55.18 million (Dec 2025: $53.5 million).
$400 million Revolving credit facility
The RCF was refinanced to $400 million on January 31, 2026. All in pricing improved to SOFR + 4.5% from SOFR + 5.26% (including a credit
adjustment spread). The facility is no longer amortising and includes a bullet repayment at final maturity, with maturity extended to 30 September
2029, i.e., maturing before the $650 million senior notes. The facility is currently fully undrawn.
$300 million Advance payment facility
The $300 million APF was closed in December 2024 to provide financing for the completion of the MPNU acquisition. The facility was fully drawn;
however, the Group has repaid and cancelled $200M under the APF in May and June 2026. The current outstanding drawdown amount is $100
million.
The APF bears interest at a rate of the aggregate of Term SOFR (including a credit adjustment spread of 0.25% per annum) plus 5% per annum
and matures in December 2027. The amortised cost for the APF as at the reporting period is $99.05 million (Dec 2025: $302.3 million).

25.1. Share-based payment liability
The Group has granted share-based awards to employees that will be settled in cash. These awards comprise the 2024 LTIP, 2025 LTIP and
2026 LTIP. Awards granted under these LTIP schemes to employees below Board level will be settled in cash and have a three year vesting
period. Share-based payment liability represents the Group’s obligation in respect of these cash-settled LTIP awards granted to employees
below Board level.
